# CLAUDE.md — Stadia Controller firmware RE

Orientation for working in this repo. Deep technical findings live in `analysis/*.md`; read those for detail rather than duplicating them here. If you're picking this project up fresh (e.g. a different model taking over for a stretch), also read `HANDOFF.md` — it explains the multi-session orchestration workflow that's been producing progress here, not just the technical state.

## What this is
This started as a dump of the Google Stadia Controller's official firmware (`bruce_*` = Bluetooth-mode, plus flashloaders). The active work is **reverse-engineering and documenting that firmware** — a preservation effort the owner cares about in its own right. The original "make it work on a PS5 (spoofing a DualShock 4)" idea is now a secondary/aspirational angle: it's blocked at the **PlayStation console-authentication layer**, not by anything in the Stadia firmware (see `analysis/` + the detailed reasoning in Claude memory). Don't relitigate the PS5 feasibility question without new information.

Scope: this is legitimate RE of firmware the owner possesses, for understanding/preservation/personal-use compatibility. Keep it that way.

Work happens on the **`firmware-re`** branch (not `main`).

## Hardware in one line
NXP **i.MX RT106x** (Cortex-M7), execute-in-place from a 16 MB Winbond **W25Q128JW** QSPI flash mapped at **0x60000000**; Broadcom **BCM43458** BT/Wi-Fi radio; FreeRTOS; firmware is HAB-signed (RSA-4096) but **not encrypted**. Full detail in Claude memory (`stadia-hardware-baseline`) and `analysis/firmware-map.md`.

## Repo layout
- `bruce_pvt_a_prod_signed.bin`, `restricted_ivt_flashloader.bin`, `flashloader_fcb_get_vendor_id.bin` — original committed dumps (repo root).
- `reference_firmware/` — firmware recovered from the Wayback Machine (Google's asset host is dead): `bruce` dev/stage/prod (code-identical, differ only in the HAB signature block), the two `gotham` Wi-Fi OTA images (`pvt-a`/`pvt-b`), and the W25Q128JW FlexSPI config block.
- `analysis/` — all RE work:
  - `firmware-map.md` — top-level structural map; `bruce` (BT) vs `gotham` (Wi-Fi, a full embedded WebRTC cloud-streaming client).
  - `bruce-ghidra.md` — Ghidra workspace details, function/module map, re-run recipes.
  - `bruce-io-paths.md` — decompiled HID input/output + rumble + input-subsystem findings; **the current working document**.
  - `bruce-crypto.md` — the embedded BoringSSL crypto stack (SHA-512, Curve25519/Ed25519 field+point arithmetic, the generic `EVP_PKEY_ASN1_METHOD` registry) — traced bottom-up from the largest functions in the image.
  - `bruce-itcm.md` — the boot-time ITCM copy (`Reset_Handler`'s `memcpy(0x0, 0x6004081c, 0xbdf8)`) and resolved `thunk_EXT_FUN_0000xxxx` HAL primitives.
  - `bruce-decompile-status.md` — **the master tracking doc for the full-decompile goal**: data-driven attributed/decompiled coverage stats (by function count and by byte size) across all ~5,000 functions, per-source-file gap table, and ranked list of the biggest unattributed contiguous address ranges. Regenerate/re-consult this before picking the next chunk of work rather than guessing.
  - `bruce-misc-functions.md` — grab-bag write-ups for decompiled functions that don't fit an existing doc's theme yet (haptics tick-path detail, sleep driver, misc input-ctor helpers, etc.).
  - `bruce-log-buffer.md` — the codebase-wide diagnostic log/message-`Buffer` framework (session 14): a small message-buffer class + rate-limited ring-buffer emission engine called directly from 64 of 139 attributed source files — very likely the mechanism generating the `__FILE__` string leaks this whole project's attribution technique depends on. Also notes two smaller extensions of existing subsystems found the same session: more BoringSSL runtime support (`ERR_put_error`/`OPENSSL_malloc`-shaped code, extends `bruce-crypto.md`) and the GKI buffer-pool allocator underlying BTA/BTE (extends `bruce-bta-stack.md`).
  - `bruce-state-machine.md` — `state_machine.cc` (session 17): a generic C++ hierarchical-state-machine (statechart) base class — run-to-completion event dispatch, least-common-ancestor transition algorithm, on-enter/on-exit virtual dispatch — plus its concrete instantiation driving the BLE adapter's pairing/advertising-mode lifecycle (ties `adapter.cc`, `advertiser.cc`, and `gatt_manager_task.cc` together).
  - `bruce-audio-subsystem.md` — (session 18) a full USB-host-mode USB Audio Class (UAC1/UAC2) driver (`usb_host_audio.cc`, `usb_host_audio_topology.cc`, `usb_audio_receive.cc`) plus a voice-path policy layer (`audio_states.cc`) that selects a "Synapse" AEC/AGC/NS backend and wires USB mic/speaker endpoints into it — almost certainly shared-platform code, not confirmed-active on the physical (BT-only, no USB-host-port) controller hardware.
  - `ghidra_scripts/` — headless GhidraScripts (Java) + `decode_hid_descriptor.py`.
  - `ghidra/` — the Ghidra project **and generated CSV/TXT outputs**. The project database (`*.gpr`, `*.rep/`) and `*.log` are **git-ignored** (regenerable); the `bruce_*.csv`/`.txt` outputs are committed.
  - `decomp/` — decompiled C for functions of interest (committed).

## Ghidra workspace — how to drive it
Ghidra 12.x (Homebrew). **Must run on JDK 21** (the system default JDK is too new and Ghidra will misbehave):
```bash
export JAVA_HOME="/opt/homebrew/opt/openjdk@21/libexec/openjdk.jdk/Contents/Home"
HL=/opt/homebrew/opt/ghidra/libexec/support/analyzeHeadless
```
Load params for any Stadia image: raw `BinaryLoader`, **base 0x60040000**, processor **`ARM:LE:32:Cortex`** (Cortex-M7 Thumb-2). (`gotham` pvt-b links at 0x60840000 instead.)

Import + analyze + map (from repo root):
```bash
"$HL" analysis/ghidra bruce -import reference_firmware/bruce_pvt_a_prod_signed.bin -overwrite \
  -loader BinaryLoader -loader-baseAddr 0x60040000 -processor ARM:LE:32:Cortex \
  -scriptPath analysis/ghidra_scripts -preScript SeedCortexM.java -postScript DumpMap.java
```
Then run scripts against the existing project without re-analyzing (`-process ... -noanalysis`):
```bash
# who references a data address (e.g. a string)
"$HL" analysis/ghidra bruce -process bruce_pvt_a_prod_signed.bin -noanalysis \
  -scriptPath analysis/ghidra_scripts -postScript FindRef.java 0x601230e7
# decompile functions -> analysis/decomp/*.c (creates a function if none exists at the addr)
"$HL" analysis/ghidra bruce -process bruce_pvt_a_prod_signed.bin -noanalysis \
  -scriptPath analysis/ghidra_scripts -postScript Decompile.java 0x60073bf0 0x600656a0
```
Open the GUI with `JAVA_HOME=… /opt/homebrew/opt/ghidra/libexec/ghidraRun` and open the `analysis/ghidra` project. The scripts hardcode output paths under `/Users/kyle/Repos/stadiaRawBtFw/analysis/`; adjust if the repo moves.

Custom scripts: `SeedCortexM` (parse the Cortex-M vector table before analysis), `DumpMap` (function/string CSVs), `AnnotateFromFilenames` (attribute functions to source files via leaked `__FILE__` strings — the core navigability trick), `FindRef` (xrefs to an address), `Decompile` (dump C + callers/callees), `MapItcmAddr` (map an ITCM address 0x0000xxxx to its flash address per `bruce-itcm.md` — accepts bare offsets or `thunk_EXT_FUN_0000xxxx` names), `PrintData` (session 15: reads/resolves a data pointer at a given address — useful when Ghidra hasn't auto-resolved a string/table xref).

**Concurrency gotcha (learned the hard way, session 14):** never launch two agents that both plan to run `analyzeHeadless -process` in the same wave, even with "check for a lock/wait if busy" instructions — that heuristic doesn't reliably converge (an agent can loop indefinitely re-checking rather than proceeding). Serialize Ghidra-touching work explicitly: one Ghidra agent per wave, full stop; pair it with genuinely non-Ghidra work instead.

## Conventions & gotchas
- **Never commit the Ghidra DB** (it's ~23 MB and regenerable) — the `analysis/.gitignore` handles `*.gpr`, `*.rep/`, `*.log`. Do commit the scripts, `.md` writeups, CSV/TXT outputs, and `decomp/*.c`.
- Commit messages end with the `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8` trailer (see git log for the pattern). Work on `firmware-re`; don't commit to `main`.
- **`getImageBase()` is 0** for a raw `BinaryLoader` import — use `getMinAddress()` for the load address (this bit `SeedCortexM` once).
- Function attribution comes from leaked `__FILE__` strings, so a function's *file* is reliable but the file's *purpose* may not be — verify by reading. Known traps: **`keys.cc` is a typed config key-value store, not gamepad buttons.** **`bcm.c` is BoringSSL's `crypto/fipsmodule/bcm.c` ("BoringCrypto Module" FIPS aggregate file) — BIGNUM + generic `EC_GROUP`/`EC_POINT`/`EC_KEY` code — not a Broadcom BT chip HCI/patchram driver, despite the name** (session 9, `analysis/bruce-crypto.md`; the real Broadcom HCI transport layer is in `analysis/bruce-bta-stack.md`).
- Pervasive `thunk_EXT_FUN_0000xxxx` calls target low addresses (0x0000xxxx) — these are hot HAL/RTOS routines (I2C, queues, memcpy/memset, FreeRTOS task-yield) copied into ITCM at boot. **Resolved (session 5, `analysis/bruce-itcm.md`):** `Reset_Handler` copies flash `0x6004081c`–`0x6004c614` to ITCM `0x0`–`0xbdf8`, so `flash_addr = itcm_addr + 0x6004081c` — the bytes are already in the static image at that flash offset, no overlay needed. Use `MapItcmAddr.java` then `Decompile.java` on the resulting flash address to resolve any given thunk; 142 of 155 done so far (all distinct thunk targets — the remaining gap is duplicate veneer stubs into already-resolved targets, not unresolved code).
- The HID report descriptor is embedded at flash **0x60103BA0**; `analysis/ghidra_scripts/decode_hid_descriptor.py` decodes it.

## Current status & next targets
**Standing goal (session 6+): fully decompile and document `bruce` to the extent it could be recompiled bit-for-bit given Google's signing keys.** Per `analysis/bruce-decompile-status.md` (regenerated session 13): of ~4,995 functions, **41.4% of the image's code by byte size is now understood in some way** (up from 22.0% at session 6), climbing further still with sessions 13-15's work (BTA sweep, `tasn_dec.c`/`adapter.cc`/`keys.cc` fully decompiled, log-buffer framework). A more precise "truly unidentified, no module guess at all" figure was **47.3%** as of session 13. That doc is the live tracking checklist but is now mildly stale again (sessions 14-15 added more) — consult it, but expect the real number to be a bit higher; regenerate when convenient rather than trusting the exact figure. Treat this as a large, ongoing, multi-session effort — delegate well-scoped chunks to subagents rather than doing bulk decompilation inline, and only one agent at a time may hold the Ghidra project open (`analysis/ghidra/bruce.rep` is a single-writer file-based DB — concurrent `analyzeHeadless -process` invocations will conflict).

The BT firmware's gamepad I/O is now almost entirely mapped: HID report format (input ID 3 / rumble output ID 5), calibration, rumble/haptics, and — as of sessions 8-12 — the full button/ADC read path (`analysis/bruce-io-paths.md`). Sessions 4-7 mapped three more major subsystems: the BoringSSL crypto stack (`analysis/bruce-crypto.md`), the ITCM boot-copy blob (fully resolved, `analysis/bruce-itcm.md`), and — the big one — the statically-linked **Broadcom BTA/BTE Bluetooth stack**, located and identified with very high confidence at `0x600921b8`–`0x600c9cc4` (HCI/BTM/GATT/SDP/SMP/L2CAP/EC-crypto; 327 of 1,076 functions decompiled, ~46% by byte size — `analysis/bruce-bta-stack.md`). Open threads, in rough priority:
1. **Report-packing function — 95% closed; the last hop is now genuinely exhausted for headless work (confirmed session 16).** Five sessions (8-12) fully traced the input path: `InputTask` (RAM `0x200108a8`, `xTaskCreateStatic`'d from `main__60051240`) polls 17 GPIO pins on a `HidPollMs` timer (`FUN_600432e8` → `FUN_60043028` → `FUN_60042ee0` → `FUN_600494e6`, confirmed debounced + active-low-inverted — buttons are **polled, not IRQ-driven**, despite a real IRQ-callback mechanism sessions 8-9 mapped that turned out to be unused/no-op'd for this purpose), samples calibrated ADC axes + 2 trigger-threshold booleans in the same function, and bit-packs everything via `FUN_60042d44` into a 16-slot ring buffer. **The one remaining hop** — what actually reads that ring buffer and calls the confirmed transmit function `hid_input_target__60058aa8` — traces to a GATT characteristic-callback slot (`thunk_FUN_600df4b0`, installed by `FUN_600813cc` on a GATT-ish object from `gatt_server__60081ab4`) whose runtime invoker resisted three sessions of headless static-analysis techniques (literal scan, function-boundary correction, generic-engine tracing) — consistent with real virtual dispatch through an untyped C++ object. **Session 15's CCCD lead (`adapter.cc`'s subscribe/unsubscribe virtual dispatch) was chased down in session 16 and closed as a dead end**: found its caller (`FUN_60069dd4`, a real BTA_GATTS-event dispatcher, itself uncalled by anything static analysis can find) and its vtable-dispatch target (the `adapter.cc` singleton's own BD_ADDR/readiness field, RAM `0x20007bf8+0x110` — confirmed, byte-level, to be a *third* object distinct from both `0x2000385c` and `gatt_server__60081ab4`'s `0x20003a60`) — it does not connect to the transmit chain. Four independent leads across four sessions (10, 11, 12, 16) have now each hit a confirmed dead end or an invisible-indirect-call wall. **This needs interactive Ghidra GUI work with manual struct-typing to close — headless sessions on this specific sub-thread are no longer a good use of time.** Confirmed useful negative: no HOGP/HID-over-GATT strings exist anywhere in the image. Full chain + all evidence in `analysis/bruce-io-paths.md` sessions 8-12 and 16, and `analysis/bruce-bta-stack.md`'s `adapter.cc` section (session 15).
2. ~~Find the real main event/dispatch loop~~ — **resolved for the BT stack (session 7):** `FUN_600a9fce` is `btu_task`, BTE's GKI-mailbox message pump (self-logs `"btu_task"`; dispatches HCI events and ACL/L2CAP data). The overall RTOS scheduler-level main loop (if distinct from this) is still unconfirmed, but the BT-stack dispatch loop specifically is done — the earlier `FUN_6004cdb8` "biggest unread function" lead was a dead end (SHA-512 compression, see `analysis/bruce-crypto.md`).
3. ~~Map the ITCM blob~~ — done (sessions 5–6): boot copy is `memcpy(0x0, 0x6004081c, 0xbdf8)`; all 142 distinct veneer targets resolved (`analysis/bruce-itcm.md`). Surfaced a previously-unknown on-device microphone/audio DSP subsystem (FFT, IIR/CIC filters, WebRTC-style noise-suppression gain control — shared with `gotham`, likely for the Assistant-button mic) plus the runtime FlexSPI/QSPI flash driver and the FreeRTOS PendSV/scheduler core.
4. ~~Find what calls into the EC crypto stack~~ — **resolved (session 7):** traced `smp_data_ind` → `smp_sm_event` (SMP's FSM dispatcher) → BLE-RAND-seeded ECDH keypair generation → confirmed EC scalar multiplication → the P-256/P-192 modular-reduction helpers, end-to-end. SMP's LE Secure Connections pairing is the real runtime consumer of the P-256 stack found in item 5 below — a more likely "live" curve than the Ed25519/X25519 stack in `analysis/bruce-crypto.md`, which still has no confirmed caller.
5. **Continue the BTA/BTE stack sweep** (`analysis/bruce-bta-stack.md`) — 418+ of 1,076 functions decompiled as of session 13 (~64% by byte size), climbing further with session 15's `adapter.cc` work. The leaked-string-table identification lead is now exhausted; remaining functions need shape/call-graph inference (lower confidence per function — QA has been holding up well on this so far, keep pairing new sweep waves with QA passes). L2CAP is located (`0x600b5xxx`–`0x600b9fxx`) and its channel-state-machine gaps filled (session 13). BTA_GATTS server API surface substantially covered.
6. ~~`bcm.c` cheap win~~ — **done (session 9):** all 52 functions decompiled and read. Turned out to be a misattribution, not a Broadcom BT driver — it's BoringSSL's BIGNUM + generic EC (`EC_GROUP`/`EC_POINT`/`EC_KEY`) library code (`analysis/bruce-crypto.md`'s session-9 section has the full function map and evidence).
7. `FUN_601054dc` (`analysis/bruce-ghidra.md`) is confirmed (session 6) to be genuine disassembly corruption — likely an unrecognized Thumb-2 `TBB`/`TBH` jump table — not a real function boundary. Needs a manual jump-table fixup in Ghidra before it's usable, not another blind decompile attempt.

9. Longer term: apply the same workflow to the `gotham` Wi-Fi firmware (the cloud-streaming stack).
10. **The codebase-wide log/diagnostic `Buffer` framework** (`analysis/bruce-log-buffer.md`, session 14) — a message-buffer class + rate-limited ring-buffer emission engine called from 64 of 139 attributed source files (widest call-graph reach found so far), very likely the source of the `__FILE__` leaks the whole attribution methodology depends on. **Session 15 partially traced the transport**: `StreamLogBuffer` is a real RPC command (16-entry dispatch table at flash `0x6013cd90`-`0x6013cec8`) whose handler (`0x6005872c`) replays the ring buffer through a generic, vtable-based, per-RPC-channel transport fan-out (`FUN_60101302`) shared by all "cwy"-framework RPC replies — not log-specific. **Still open**: where that vtable's transport objects get registered (UART vs USB CDC-ACM vs BLE — `uart.cc` and `usb_device_cdc_acm.c` both exist as attributed candidates, unconfirmed). Confirmed this does NOT close the separate "map the vendor USB interface, class 0xFF bulk EP7" open thread from `analysis/firmware-map.md` (CDC-ACM is a different, standard USB class).

`system_flag_equal_0` is a real predicate but at `+8`/16-bit, not `+4`/8-bit; `system_flag_equal_2` and `system_state_byte_getter` are both `void`, no-return struct-clearing/reset helpers, not predicates/getters — the whole cluster reads as a "same-shaped sibling functions must all be simple checks" assumption never checked against the actual bodies (exactly the anti-pattern HANDOFF Rule 5 targets). **Radio/Clock cluster (6/6 wrong, cross-referenced against `bruce-itcm.md`)**: `hardware_radio_power_config` has **0 callees per its own header** — it's a self-contained linked-list countdown/expiry walker (timer-tick shape), not RF-clock gating, and makes none of the three claimed thunk calls; all four external thunk targets it was claimed to gate (`0000714c`, `00007d64`, `00007dac`, `00007f58`) are already independently resolved in `bruce-itcm.md` since session 5/6 as, respectively, a tick-count getter, `portENTER_CRITICAL()`, `portEXIT_CRITICAL()`, and the `pvPortMalloc` free-list search (`heap_5_improved.c`) — none are power/clock/crystal related, and the `00007f58`/heap-allocator identity is independently double-confirmed by session 61's `heap_alloc_thunk` using the same target; `clock_gate_disable_thunk` calls the already-established PendSV-trigger `0000737c` (`bruce-io-paths.md`), not `00007dac` — consistent with its real caller this same wave (`flash_page_verify_chunk`'s 100-iteration poll loop) using it as a yield/delay, not a clock op. **Flash cluster (1 softened, 1 count-corrected, 1 clean)**: `flash_page_verify_chunk`'s "verifies against expected data" isn't evidenced by its actual wait-then-fetch poll-loop body — softened to unconfirmed rather than corrected, since no confident alternative identity is evidenced either; `flash_status_callback_dispatch` claimed 0 callees but the body has 2 (`FUN_6006e9b0()` plus the claimed `FUN_6006e854`); `flash_stream_write_loop` and `sensor_i2c_read_and_calibrate` and `thunk_FUN_6006f088` are the only 3 of 20 rows confirmed clean on inspection. **File-collision data gap**: `0x600d9378` and `0x600d937c` have no dedicated `.c` file — both were decompiled with target-only filenames (`thunk_EXT_FUN_00007d64.c`, `thunk_EXT_FUN_00007dac.c`) instead of the collision-safe `thunk_<name>__<addr>.c` convention session 57 introduced specifically to prevent this, so each file now only reflects its *second* colliding source address's occurrence — meaning this wave actually added 18 distinct files, not 20 (lower severity than session 56's `private_heap_free` incident since both collided addresses are brand-new genuine duplicate veneers into the same real target, so no pre-existing content was lost). All findings corrected/flagged in place in `bruce-misc-functions.md`; `GHIDRA-TODO: re-decompile 0x600d9378 and 0x600d937c with disambiguated filenames, and investigate why the session-57 naming fix didn't apply to this wave's thunk decompiles`. Independently re-derived `bruce-decompile-status.md`'s totals (2,232 functions / 404,942 bytes, 0 mismatches) — match exactly; the doc's own bookkeeping is unaffected by the wave's inflated function count since it counts files on disk, not the wave's self-reported number. **Reliability read**: a sharp, isolated regression after five consecutive strong-to-clean waves (61-65) — the error pattern here (assuming behavior from address/name proximity to siblings, and from what "should" be true of a plausible-sounding subsystem, rather than reading each decompiled body) is exactly what HANDOFF's rigor rules were written to catch; worth flagging explicitly to the next wave as a reminder that trivial-looking, tightly-clustered small functions still need individual verification, not pattern-matched guesses.

123. **[QA] session 82 (independent) — one confirmed numeric error caught via raw disassembly, rest verified clean.** Backbone clean: all 20 addresses/sizes match the census exactly, summing to the claimed 1,004 bytes, no overlaps. **Confirmed wrong**: `synapse_audio_aec_cross_correlation_matrix_alloc`'s claimed inverse-scale computation "`1.0/(param_3-1)`" — disassembled the function directly (`vmov s15,r2; vcvt.f32.u32 s15,s15; ...; vdiv.f32 s14,s13,s15` all execute *before* `subs r5,r2,#1` computes the decremented value used elsewhere in the same function) and confirmed the VFP division genuinely operates on the original, unmodified `param_3` — the inverse scale is `1.0/param_3`, not `1.0/(param_3-1)`. 127. **[QA] session 84 (independent) — zero corrections; the protobuf varint encoder is a bit-exact, unambiguous match — arguably the strongest single confirmation this whole pipeline has produced.** Backbone clean: all 20 addresses/sizes match the census exactly, summing to the claimed 936 bytes, no overlaps. `protobuf_encode_varint64` was read in full rather than trusted on the strength of its name: it correctly implements the single-byte fast path for values `< 0x80`; the general-case loop writes 7-bit chunks with the continuation bit set (`| 0x80`) on every byte except the last, where the same slot gets overwritten with the bit cleared (`& 0x7f`) after the loop exits — the exact LEB128 termination rule; and the 64-bit right-shift-by-7 is correctly implemented across the two 32-bit halves (`param_3 = param_3>>7 | param_4<<25`, `param_4 = param_4>>7`), i.e. genuinely correct multi-word bit arithmetic, not just plausible-looking code. `protobuf_encode_tag`'s formula reduces to the real `tag = (field_number << 3) | wire_type`, including the correct high-word overflow handling (`field_number >> 29`) for when the shift would spill past 32 bits. `protobuf_buffer_append_bytes` and `power_state_machine_send_command_13` (opcode `0xd`, target offset `+0x2188`) both matched exactly. Independently re-derived `bruce-decompile-status.md`'s totals (2,594 functions / 425,286 bytes, 0 mismatches) via a fresh header-parsed join — match exactly. **Reliability read**: this wave surfaces a genuinely notable finding for the project — a real embedded Protocol Buffers serialization runtime, evidenced at the bit level, not just by plausible naming; worth flagging as one of the more interesting discoveries in the whole decompile effort.

129. **[QA] session 85 (independent) — zero corrections; the zigzag decoder's core arithmetic verified bit-exact, continuing session 84's protobuf confirmation.** Backbone clean: all 20 addresses/sizes match the census exactly, summing to the claimed 1,030 bytes, no overlaps. `protobuf_decode_sint64_zigzag`'s math was read in full: the 64-bit right-shift-by-1 across two 32-bit words (`(high&1)<<31 | low>>1`) is correct multi-word arithmetic, and the sign branch (`low_bit==0` → output the shifted value directly; `low_bit==1` → output its bitwise NOT) is an exactly equivalent, branch-based formulation of the canonical zigzag decode formula `(n>>1) ^ -(n&1)` (since XOR with all-ones equals NOT) — confirmed correct, not just plausible. One residual oddity noted but not flagged as an error: the same register (`param_2`) appears to serve double duty as both the 64-bit input's high word and the output pointer, which reads as an artifact of how the decompiler is presenting register reuse rather than a wrong behavioral claim — the math itself doesn't depend on resolving that ambiguity. `protobuf_decode_tag_and_wire_type`'s `tag>>3`/`tag&7` field-number/wire-type split matches exactly, and `remote_device_db_lookup_channel_11`'s literal channel-type match (`'\v'` = `0xb` = 11) is exact. Independently re-derived `bruce-decompile-status.md`'s totals (2,614 functions / 426,316 bytes, 0 mismatches) via a fresh header-parsed join — match exactly. **Reliability read**: second consecutive wave with a bit-level-verified protobuf primitive — the encoder (session 84) and now the zigzag decoder both check out precisely, reinforcing that this is a genuine, well-evidenced protobuf runtime rather than a superficial naming pattern.

135. **[QA] session 88 (independent) — headline finding overturns an ~80-session-old (session 4/5-era) conclusion in `bruce-crypto.md`; a second, smaller mislabel corrected in `bruce-misc-functions.md`.** Backbone check surfaced a genuine census overlap between this wave's new `0x600e06f6` and a pre-existing decomp file at `0x600e06ee` (committed in session 4/5, commit `c997b40`, long before the "tail-call bleed" artifact class was even recognized as a pattern, let alone routinely checked for). Disassembled the raw bytes at `0x600e06d0`/`0x600e06ee`/`0x600e06f6` directly: `FUN_600e06ee.c`'s committed body (claimed 30 bytes, calling `evp__6008506c` directly) is a stale tail-call-bleed artifact — the function's real size is 8 bytes (`mov r2,r1; movs r1,#0x74; b.w 0x600e06d0`), a trampoline into a shared, currently-undefined dispatcher at `0x600e06d0` (real body ends `0x600e06e4`). That dispatcher has (at least) 3 confirmed sibling trampolines carrying different NID constants: `0x600e06e6` (code `6`=RSA), `0x600e06ee` (code `0x74`=DSA, the stale one), and this wave's own `0x600e06f6` (code `0x198`=EC). Critically, `0x600e06f6`'s trampoline has a **confirmed real caller**, `FUN_60085528` (180 bytes, not yet independently traced) — this directly refutes `bruce-crypto.md`'s long-standing conclusion that the whole `EVP_PKEY_ASN1_METHOD`/ASN.1 registry is a dead end with "no callers found" and "exactly one caller" (`FUN_600e06ee`, itself uncalled). Corrected `analysis/bruce-crypto.md` in place: struck the old "no live call path" conclusion, preserved it as "Prior conclusion (superseded)" for history, and added a fully-evidenced ⚠️ REFUTED section with the disassembly proof and a `GHIDRA-TODO` (define a Function boundary at `0x600e06d0`, mark the 3 tail-call edges as `CALL_RETURN` overrides via `FixTailCalls.java`, then re-decompile `0x600e06ee` at its true 8-byte size). Ed25519/X25519 (NIDs `0x3b5`/`0x3b4`) are unaffected by this specific correction and remain unconfirmed, since neither NID appeared among the 3 trampolines found so far. **Second finding**: `crypto_x25519_key_memcmp_32b` (`0x600e0914`) was labeled "constant-time" 32-byte key comparison, but its sole callee is `thunk_EXT_FUN_0000b554`, independently established since session 5 (`bruce-itcm.md`) and re-confirmed at 5+ other sites across `bruce-bta-stack.md`/`bruce-misc-functions.md` as plain, early-exiting `memcmp` — the opposite property. This is directly contrasted, within the same wave, by `crypto_constant_time_memcmp_diff` (`0x600e0976`), which really does show the defining no-early-exit XOR-accumulate shape of constant-time comparison — read and confirmed this session. Corrected the `0x600e0914` row in `analysis/bruce-misc-functions.md` in place; the 32-byte-buffer and true-if-identical behavior claims were otherwise accurate. Independently re-derived `bruce-decompile-status.md`'s totals via a fresh header-parsed census join (2,674 functions / 428,836 bytes, 0 mismatches, 0 non-census addresses) — matches the wave's claimed totals exactly. **Reliability read**: this wave's own 18 other functions read clean, but the two findings here are more consequential than a typical session's corrections — one overturns a foundational analytical claim about live-vs-dead code that has stood unquestioned since the project's earliest sessions, a reminder that even "closed" conclusions from before the tail-call-bleed artifact class was cataloged (sessions 30+) are worth re-checking whenever new work touches their address ranges.

139. **[QA] session 90 (independent) — the Curve25519/Ed25519 field-arithmetic core is bit-exact and exceptionally well corroborated; but the entire 4-function "Ed25519 group operations" cluster, plus 2 OpenSSL-stack functions, are misidentified (6 confirmed corrections).** Backbone clean: all 20 addresses/sizes match the census exactly, summing to the claimed 3,936 bytes, no overlaps; independently re-derived `bruce-decompile-status.md`'s totals via a fresh header-parsed join (2,714 functions / 433,170 bytes, 0 mismatches, 0 non-census addresses) — matches the wave's claimed 60.59% exactly. Gave the field-arithmetic layer (`fe25519_*`, 10 of 20 functions, including the wave's two largest at 1190B/1160B) full Rule-9 defining-behavior treatment, and it is now among the strongest-verified claims in the whole pipeline: `fe25519_mul`'s 10x10-limb cross-product structure with reduction constants `0x13`(19)/`0x26`(38) and the alternating 26/25-bit carry masks (`0x3ffffff`/`0x1ffffff`) is a line-for-line structural match to the real ref10 `fe_mul`; `fe25519_sq` shows the exact squaring-specific optimization (doubled cross-terms folded into single 64-bit products) that distinguishes real `fe_sq` from a generic multiply; `fe25519_invert`'s addition-chain loop-iteration-count sequence — **4, 9, 19, 9, 49, 99, 49, 4** — is a bit-exact match to the famous, distinctive `2^5,2^10,2^20,2^40,2^50,2^100,2^200,2^250,2^255` Fermat-inversion addition chain published in every ref10-derived Curve25519 implementation; `fe25519_sub`'s three bias constants (`0x7ffffda`, `0x3fffffe`, `0x7fffffe`) are the exact `2·2^26−38`/`2·2^25−2`/`2·2^26−2` per-limb-position bias values from the reference 32-bit unsigned-limb `fe25519_sub`; and `fe25519_cmov`'s branchless `out = in ^ ((in^from) & −b)` masking, `fe25519_frombytes`'s 32-byte little-endian unpack into the same 26/25-bit radix, and `fe25519_0`/`fe25519_1`/`fe25519_add`/`fe25519_carry` all matched their claims exactly. **But the 4-function "Ed25519 Projective & Cached Point Operations" cluster built on top of this correct field layer is entirely misidentified** — all 4 rows follow plausible standard ref10 function *names* (`ge_p2_dbl`, `ge_p3_0`, `ge_p3_to_p2`, `ge_p3_to_cached`) without checking each body against its real formula: (1) `ge25519_p2_dbl` (146B) takes **3** parameters and calls `fe25519_mul` — real point-doubling takes 1 input and uses only squarings; its actual `Y+X`/`Y−X`-of-P-then-multiply-against-Q's-fields shape is the defining signature of extended-point **addition** (`ge_add`/`ge_madd` family), not doubling. (2) `ge25519_p3_0` (20B) is **byte-for-byte identical** to this same wave's own, correctly-labeled `fe25519_1` (`0x600e1c08`) — it sets one 40-byte field element to 1, not a 160-byte four-field point identity; likely a second compiled instance of the same primitive, real point-identity setup probably lives in the shared caller `FUN_60087964`. (3) `ge25519_p3_to_p2` (74B) does invert `Z` and compute `X/Z`,`Y/Z` as claimed, but then packs `Y/Z` to 32 bytes and XORs the sign bit of `X/Z` into the top output bit — the unmistakable signature of Ed25519 **point compression** (`ge_p3_tobytes`), not a bare coordinate-format conversion. (4) `ge25519_p3_to_cached` (66B) has **zero** add/sub calls and no curve-constant multiply (real `ge_p3_to_cached` needs both); its actual four products `X·T, Y·Z, Z·T, X·Y`, in that exact order, are a bit-exact match to the real ref10 **`ge_p1p1_to_p3`** formula. **Two more findings in the OpenSSL-stack cluster**: `crypto_stack_sort_and_find` (38B) has no sort call and no binary-search loop — real behavior is a bounds-checked (rejects negative/out-of-range index) direct indexed getter, a stricter sibling of `crypto_stack_value_at_index`; `crypto_stack_is_sorted` (6B)'s claimed formula `*(stack+8)` is wrong on both the offset and the indirection depth — raw disassembly (`ldr r3,[r0]; ldr r0,[r3,#0x14]; bx lr`) confirms `*(*param_1+0x14)`, and since the established stack header is only `0x14` bytes total, this address is likely a `device_info.cc`-specific field getter (its sole caller) that only superficially resembles a stack accessor, not confirmed as `OPENSSL_sk_is_sorted` at all. All 6 corrected in place in `analysis/bruce-misc-functions.md` with full evidence; no `GHIDRA-TODO`s needed (identification-only, no boundary issues). **Reliability read**: a genuinely bifurcated wave — the numerically dense field-arithmetic core got the deepest, most rigorous verification of any claim class this pipeline has attempted (rivaling sessions 72's rb-tree and 78's FFT matches) and passed with flying colors, while the smaller, shape-pattern-matched point-operation and stack-helper functions built on top of it show the exact "plausible standard name assigned without reading the body against its real formula" failure mode session 66 was flagged for — a reminder that strong verification of a cluster's foundation doesn't transfer to claims about what's built on it.

141. **[QA] session 91 (independent) — `ge25519_add_p3_cached` resolves last session's open `ge_add`-vs-`ge_madd` question by cross-comparison; but the wave's "Curve25519 Montgomery ladder" cluster (its 3 largest, most prominent functions, 4,114 of 6,354 bytes) is entirely misidentified — it is 256-bit scalar arithmetic, not point/field arithmetic.** Backbone clean: all 20 addresses/sizes match the census exactly, summing to the claimed 6,354 bytes, no overlaps; independently re-derived `bruce-decompile-status.md`'s totals via a fresh header-parsed join (2,734 functions / 439,524 bytes, 0 mismatches, 0 non-census addresses) — matches the wave's claimed 61.48% exactly. **Positive cross-session finding**: `ge25519_add_p3_cached` (`0x600e1cd4`) is structurally near-identical to session 90's mis-labeled `ge25519_p2_dbl` (`0x600e1b5a`, corrected to "extended-point + cached-point addition, exact ref10 variant unconfirmed") — the only difference is this function multiplies `P.Z·Q.Z` (doubled) where `0x600e1b5a` self-doubles `P.Z` with no multiply, exactly the documented `ge_add` (full 4-field cached point) vs `ge_madd` (3-field precomp, `Q.Z` implicitly 1) distinction. This resolves the open question: `0x600e1b5a` is **`ge25519_madd`**, `0x600e1cd4` is the full **`ge25519_add`** — both confirmed bit-exact against ref10's real formulas field-by-field. Sharpened in `bruce-misc-functions.md`'s session-90 row. **The major finding**: the wave's headline "Curve25519 Montgomery ladder" trio doesn't hold up. `crypto_curve25519_ladder_step` (642B)'s **only callee is `crypto_constant_time_select_u32`** — zero `fe25519_*` calls; its body is a full 8-word (256-bit) carry-chain **addition** with an `&0xff`-masked conditional-subtract-and-select correction, the defining shape of constant-time scalar addition mod something, not a Montgomery ladder step (which is *defined* by `fe_add`/`fe_sub`/`fe_mul`/`fe_sq` on 10-limb field elements). `crypto_curve25519_cswap_and_add` (474B) has **no callees and no swap logic at all** — it's an 8-word **subtraction** with the identical conditional-correction idiom, sharing *both* callers exactly with the mis-labeled ladder-step. Most strikingly, `crypto_ed25519_scalarmult_variable_base` (2998B — the largest single function this whole pipeline has decompiled) has, across its entire body, **the same single callee** (`crypto_constant_time_select_u32`) and **zero** calls to any `fe25519_*` or `ge25519_*` function — structurally disqualifying for "elliptic-curve scalar multiplication," which cannot be implemented without repeated point doubling/addition through the field layer. It shares its exact caller pair with `crypto_ed25519_scalar_frombytes` (confirmed correct this session — cleanly unpacks 32 bytes into 8 words), reinforcing that all three functions operate purely in the 256-bit **scalar** (word-array, mod-`L`) domain. Real identities left as "256-bit scalar add/sub-with-conditional-reduce" and "large multi-precision scalar modular arithmetic (plausibly multiply-and-reduce mod `L`)" rather than guessed ref10 names. All 3 corrected in place in `analysis/bruce-misc-functions.md` with full evidence; no `GHIDRA-TODO`s needed. Spot-checked the remainder: `crypto_constant_time_is_zero_u32`/`_select_u32` are bit-exact standard constant-time idioms; `crypto_xor_128b` (16 bytes, 4 words) matches its claim exactly, but `crypto_xor_words_16b` actually XORs **32 bytes** (8 words, loop bound `0x1c`→8 iterations) despite its name and description both claiming 16 — flagged inline as a minor size-naming mismatch (not struck through, since the mechanism itself is correctly described as an in-place word XOR); `x25519_key_derive_public`'s RFC 7748 scalar clamping (`k[0]&=0xf8; k[31]&=0x7f; k[31]|=0x40`) is bit-exact; `crypto_ed25519_scalar_frombytes`'s 32-byte→8-word little-endian unpack is exact; and `crypto_aes_bitsliced_sbox`'s pure straight-line bitwise-gate body (27 temporaries, no table lookup, no loop) structurally supports a genuine Boyar-Peralta-style boolean-circuit S-box. **Reliability read**: a second consecutive wave (after session 90) where a numerically-dense field-arithmetic/bit-utility layer is decompiled with high mechanical precision while a prominent, plausible-sounding cluster of names built on top (this time "Montgomery ladder," picked because the surrounding functions are genuinely Curve25519-related) turns out to describe the wrong mathematical domain entirely (scalar vs. point/field) — the single most load-bearing check available for this class of claim is simply "does this function call the field/point layer it would need to," which caught all three here immediately.

143. **[QA] session 92 (independent) — zero confirmed-wrong claims, but new evidence sharpens session 91's open question into a well-defined research thread: this whole "Ed25519 scalarmult" cluster is a structurally complete, internally consistent 3-coordinate arithmetic engine that never touches the established `fe25519_*`/`ge25519_*` stack.** Backbone clean: all 20 addresses/sizes match the census exactly, summing to the claimed 1,434 bytes, no overlaps; independently re-derived `bruce-decompile-status.md`'s totals via a fresh header-parsed join (2,754 functions / 440,958 bytes, 0 mismatches, 0 non-census addresses) — matches the wave's claimed 61.68% exactly. All 20 functions read/spot-checked; every literal behavioral claim held up (`crypto_ed25519_scalar_is_zero_256`'s 8-word OR-reduction, `_copy_256`'s 8-word copy loop, `crypto_heap_free_checked`'s exact 14-caller count, `crypto_get_bit_at_index`'s bounds-checked bit extract, `crypto_aes_bitsliced_sbox_transpose`'s transpose→sbox→untranspose sandwich around the session-91-confirmed S-box, all confirmed exactly). **The key contribution is contextual, not corrective**: `crypto_ed25519_scalarmult_step_add` (`0x600e68ac`) and `crypto_ed25519_scalar_pack_step` (`0x600e693e`) both unpack **3** separate 0x44-byte-strided sub-fields of their input into 32-byte/8-word buffers (via the confirmed `crypto_ed25519_scalar_frombytes`) and hand them to an inner step function — and those inner functions (`FUN_600e66be`, `FUN_600e65d2`) are the *exact same* two functions session 91 found calling the mis-labeled "Montgomery ladder" primitives. This directly confirms a 3-coordinate ("point"-shaped) structure is being manipulated entirely through the 8-word scalar toolkit. Crucially, `crypto_ed25519_scalar_table_lookup` (`0x600e69c2`) is **confirmed correct and precisely implemented** — a genuine constant-time lookup (XOR-index-then-cmov-touch-every-entry) over a table of 3×32-byte entries, real windowed-scalarmult-table infrastructure. So the cluster is *not* garbage or a dead end — it's coherent, complete (table lookup + cmov + is_zero + copy + pack/unpack + add-mod + sub-mod + select) infrastructure for multiplying a scalar by a 3-coordinate point — it just isn't built on the `fe25519_*`/`ge25519_*` Curve25519/Ed25519 layer sessions 90–91 independently verified bit-exact. Flagged as an open question (not struck through, since neither hypothesis is confirmed) on `crypto_curve25519_ladder_step`'s row in `analysis/bruce-misc-functions.md`: either (a) a separate/portable 8-word Curve25519 or Ed25519-scalar implementation variant, or (b) BoringSSL's generic `EC_POINT`/`bcm.c`-style scalar-multiplication code for an unrelated curve, misattributed to "Ed25519" by thematic/address proximity alone — recommended next step is checking the add/sub-with-conditional-correction routines' modulus literals against known Curve25519/Ed25519-`L` vs. P-256/other-curve constants. **Reliability read**: a good example of QA adding value beyond error-correction — no new strikethroughs this wave, but three sessions of accumulated structural evidence (91, 92) have turned a vague "this doesn't look like curve arithmetic" observation into a precise, falsifiable, next-session-actionable question.

145. **[QA] session 93 (independent) — the AES-GCM cluster is exceptionally well corroborated by 3 sessions of prior confirmed primitives; but 3 of 15 "BIGNUM" functions are misidentified generic constant-time helpers, one directly and satisfyingly cross-validated against a session-9 `bruce-crypto.md` finding from 84 sessions ago; plus an unrelated stale-boundary regression caught by the routine backbone check.** Backbone: all 20 new addresses/sizes match the census exactly, summing to the claimed 1,506 bytes, no overlaps. **Found one pre-existing, off-wave regression during the routine full-repository mismatch scan**: `bcm__6008c0d8.c` (a session-9-era decomp file, `src: bcm.c`, unrelated to this wave's own address range) claims 628 bytes in its own header but the current census now shows 600 — `git log -p` on `bruce_functions.csv` confirms the 628→600 change landed in *this session's own commit*, despite not being mentioned anywhere in the commit message. Corrected the byte count in `analysis/bruce-crypto.md`'s modexp table with a `GHIDRA-TODO` to re-decompile `bcm__6008c0d8` at 600 bytes; the wave's own claimed 442,436-byte total already uses the correct (census) size, so no total needed fixing. **AES-GCM cluster (5 functions) — strong, multi-session cross-validation, zero corrections**: `crypto_aes_gcm_encrypt_blocks` correctly composes THREE independently-confirmed session-91/92 primitives (`crypto_aes_bitsliced_pack_state`/`_unpack_state`, `crypto_xor_128b`) into a coherent CTR-mode-style block-counter loop with GCM's characteristic big-endian counter byte-swapping; `crypto_aes_bitsliced_full_round_decrypt` composes *six* confirmed session-91 bitsliced-transform primitives (S-box, ShiftRows, MixColumns, rotate, `crypto_xor_words_16b`) into a plausible per-round decrypt loop; `crypto_aes_gcm_decrypt_blocks` reuses the same building blocks across two sub-mode branches. **BIGNUM cluster (15 functions) — 12 confirmed correct with strong internal offset-consistency (e.g. the BIGNUM "neg" field at struct offset `0xc` and the 20-byte struct-size convention both independently confirmed by 3+ separate functions), but 3 misidentified**: (1) `crypto_bn_add_words_with_carry` (158B) has no `+`/carry logic anywhere and writes no output array — it's a **constant-time multi-word BIGNUM comparison** (is-zero-masked propagate-on-first-difference, plus a length-mismatch tail check), not addition. (2) `crypto_bn_set_bit` (102B) doesn't twiddle a single bit — it zeroes a destination and OR-accumulates the matching entry from a `2^param_5`-sized table via an `is_zero`-masked select, i.e. a **constant-time precomputed-table selector**. This one has a genuinely satisfying independent cross-check: its sole caller, `bcm__6008c334`, was already documented in `bruce-crypto.md` since **session 9** as `BN_mod_exp_mont_consttime`-shaped with a "fixed-width table-gather" step — precisely what this function turns out to be, confirmed from a completely independent angle 84 sessions later. But each function's scope was drastically understated: sandwiched between the `IP` and `FP` halves, both call the shared Feistel round-function `FUN_6008893c` **three times** — `0x600ea032` with mode flags `(1,0,1)` (Encrypt-Decrypt-Encrypt, the classic **3DES-EDE** construction) and `0x600ea0e8` with the exact inverse `(0,1,0)` (Decrypt-Encrypt-Decrypt). Independently re-derived `bruce-decompile-status.md`'s totals via a fresh header-parsed join (2,893 functions / 455,660 bytes) — matches the wave's claimed 63.75% exactly. **Main finding**: `crypto_ec_point_get_x` (`0x600ea886`, `return *param_1`) and `crypto_ec_point_get_y` (`0x600ea88e`, `return *(param_1+4)`) are each 4-byte, single-instruction bodies whose actual observed callers are **not** EC-related — `crypto_ed25519_pubkey_import`, `crypto_ed25519_key_decode`, and `crypto_ed25519_key_compare` (all already independently attributed, in this same file, to Ed25519 key-material access, with one row explicitly describing this exact function as extracting "key material lengths and pointers") — and `crypto_ec_group_get_generator_offset` (`0x600ea7fa`, `return param_1+8`) is *also* already cited by an existing row as part of a **Gotham connection-cleanup cascade**, a third, wholly unrelated context. Flagged with confidence caveats in place (not struck through, since a real EC use *might* also exist among the untraced callers) on all three rows in `analysis/bruce-misc-functions.md`; `crypto_ec_point_get_z` was left with only a lighter positive note, since its one known caller genuinely is EC/ASN.1-attributed, giving it comparatively better support. **Confirmed correct**: `crypto_ecdsa_sig_new`/`crypto_ecdsa_sig_free` — a clean allocate-two-BIGNUMs/free-two-BIGNUMs pair matching real `ECDSA_SIG_new`/`_free` exactly, correctly composing the confirmed BIGNUM allocator (session 90) and checked-free (session 92) primitives. **Reliability read**: a structurally different kind of finding than recent sessions' wrong-operation or swapped-name errors — this one surfaces a general hazard specific to *tiny* (≤8 byte) accessor functions in this codebase: their bodies are too small to carry any type-specific evidence, so identity claims for them are only as strong as the caller context, and this file's own cross-references (not fresh disassembly) were what exposed the conflict here.

Found one census mismatch during the routine full-repository scan: `bcm__6008db08.c` (already independently documented in `bruce-crypto.md` as `EC_POINT_cmp`-shaped, high confidence, predating this wave) claims 300 bytes but the census now shows 68 — `git log -p` confirms the shrink landed in *this session's own commit*, the same undocumented pattern as sessions 93/95's `bcm__6008c0d8` regressions. Disassembly of the true 68 bytes shows this function is only the **method-match guard** half (two calls to an established method-check, error-logging on mismatch); on success it genuinely **tail-calls** (`b.w`, not `bl`) `FUN_600eb370` with `+4` pointer offsets — the real "point-equality compare" logic lives there, not in this function, and the old 300-byte decompile was tail-call bleed-through. Corrected in `bruce-crypto.md` with a `GHIDRA-TODO` to re-decompile at 68 bytes; also softened session 90's `crypto_ed25519_key_compare` row, which calls this same function "a constant-time comparison helper" — the actual comparison is one tail-call further away. **Main content finding**: `crypto_ec_point_is_at_infinity` (`0x600eab3a`) has its boolean sense **inverted** — traced precisely: OR-reduce the checked field's words, then `~is_zero(OR)` via the confirmed `crypto_constant_time_is_zero_u32`; working through the algebra, this returns all-1s when the field is **nonzero** (finite) and `0` when the field is all-zero (infinity) — the exact opposite of "is at infinity." Confirmed by its own caller, `crypto_ec_point_set_to_infinity`, which uses the result directly as an AND-mask to zero a computed coordinate — a scheme that only works if the mask is `0` precisely when the point *was already* infinite, i.e. if this function is really "is-finite." Corrected in place in `analysis/bruce-misc-functions.md`. 163. **[QA] session 102 (independent) — the entire "RC4 Stream Cipher" section (3 functions) is misidentified as generic CBC-mode block-cipher glue with no RC4 algorithm anywhere; "Poly1305 Donna mul" narrowed to CMAC subkey generation.** Backbone: all 20 new addresses/sizes match the census exactly, summing to the claimed 1,728 bytes, no overlaps; confirmed `bcm__6008d6f8` is now genuinely 52 bytes, clean content, and census remains at **0 mismatches** repository-wide. Independently re-derived `bruce-decompile-status.md`'s totals via a fresh header-parsed join (2,952 functions / 461,048 bytes) — matches the wave's claimed 64.49% exactly. **Headline finding**: `crypto_rc4_set_key` (188B) and `crypto_rc4_crypt_blocks` (334B) both process data in **16-byte blocks**, chain each block against the previous one, and invoke a **caller-supplied block-cipher function pointer** on every block (the decrypt-side function additionally has explicit overlapping-buffer safety handling, saving the ciphertext block before it can be overwritten) — real RC4 has none of this: it's a byte-serial keystream generator with a 256-entry S-box and two running indices, no blocks, no IV, no block-cipher callback, and no distinction between encrypt and decrypt. This is the generic **CBC-mode encrypt/decrypt engine** (`CRYPTO_cbc128_encrypt`/`_decrypt`), parameterized by whatever block cipher is plugged in via the callback — confirmed by `crypto_rc4_cipher_wrapper` (56B), which dispatches between the two based on an encrypt/decrypt direction flag using a per-context block-cipher pointer and IV location, something RC4 (identical operation either direction) has no reason to need. All 3 corrected in place in `analysis/bruce-misc-functions.md`. **Second finding**: `crypto_poly1305_donna_mul` (102B) was hedged as "Poly1305 modular multiplication... or CMAC subkey generation" — the body only doubles a 128-bit value (carry-propagated left-shift-by-1 across 4 words) and conditionally XORs a fixed constant based on the pre-shift top bit; there's no second operand, no multiply-accumulate, and no 130-bit/5-limb arithmetic anywhere, ruling out Poly1305-donna's actual multiply. This is exactly AES-CMAC's subkey-doubling step (`K1=double(L)`, `K2=double(K1)`); narrowed to CMAC subkey generation only. **Reliability read**: the RC4 misidentification is a strong instance of a now-familiar pattern (a wrong headline label anchoring several follow-on functions' names via structural mirroring, as in session 95's add/sub cascade) — the tell here was maximally clear-cut, since "byte-serial stream cipher" and "block-cipher-callback-driven CBC engine" are about as structurally distinct as two crypto primitives can be, making this a useful example of how far a plausible section header can drift from the actual bodies once nobody re-checks it against the algorithm's defining shape.

165. **[QA] session 103 (independent) — the ASN.1 DER encoder/decoder primitives are bit-exact, professional-grade matches to the real spec; one function mislabeled "ASN.1" by coincidental naming proximity turns out to be an AES-GCM hardware-acceleration gate, already correctly described elsewhere in this same file.** Backbone: all 20 new addresses/sizes match the census exactly, summing to the claimed 1,856 bytes, no overlaps; census remains at **0 mismatches** repository-wide. Independently re-derived `bruce-decompile-status.md`'s totals via a fresh header-parsed join (2,969 functions / 462,362 bytes) — matches the wave's claimed 64.68% exactly. **Strong positive verification**: `crypto_asn1_put_object_header` (166B) is a bit-exact match to real `ASN1_put_object` — constructed-bit `0x20`, class-bits mask `0xc0`, the high-tag-number marker `0x1f` with base-128 continuation-bit encoding for tag numbers ≥31, the `0x80` indefinite-length/long-form-length markers, all present and correctly combined; `crypto_asn1_put_eoc`/`crypto_asn1_check_eoc` are an exact complementary write/read pair for the real 2-zero-byte BER End-Of-Contents marker; `crypto_asn1_is_tag_10` correctly checks for `0x10`(16), the real ASN.1 SEQUENCE tag number. **One misidentification**: `crypto_asn1_check_type_10` (16B) claimed to check "ASN.1 item type... equals 10," but its **3 callers are `crypto_aes_gcm_mode_dispatch_encrypt`/`_decrypt`** (`0x600e6dfe`/`0x600e70ba`) — both *already independently described in this same file*, since sessions 98/102, as calling this exact function to "check hardware acceleration." The checked struct is `EVP_CIPHER_CTX`-shaped and the value `10` matches real OpenSSL's `EVP_CIPH_GCM_MODE` enum constant — this is a cipher-mode/hardware-acceleration gate, not ASN.1 at all; its superficial "checks equals a small constant" resemblance to the neighboring, genuinely-ASN.1 `crypto_asn1_is_tag_10` (which checks a *different* value, `0x10`=16) is coincidental. Corrected in place in `analysis/bruce-misc-functions.md`. **Reliability read**: a clean, well-earned split — the DER-encoding primitives got some of the strongest spec-level confirmation this pipeline has produced, while the one miss was caught purely by cross-referencing this file's own earlier, already-correct rows against a newly (and wrongly) re-labeled one — the same "check the doc's own existing citations" technique that has now caught real errors in at least three separate sessions (73, 83, 103).

171. **[QA] session 106 (independent) — the largest single-wave correction count in the pipeline's history: 9 of 14 CBB functions role-swapped or misidentified, forming a fully self-consistent revised picture; the 5 CBS functions and the 3 plain `add_uN` functions were independently clean.** Backbone: all 20 new addresses/sizes match the census exactly, summing to the claimed 1,138 bytes, in one fully contiguous run (`0x600ecf48`–`0x600ed3ba`) with zero gaps/overlaps against the surrounding census; census remains at **0 mismatches** repository-wide. Independently re-derived `bruce-decompile-status.md`'s totals via a fresh header-parsed join (3,028 functions / 465,168 bytes) — matches the wave's claimed 65.10% exactly. Verified the `0x600ed380` boundary fix (14B→24B) directly via disassembly: a clean, complete `CBS_skip`-shaped bounds-check/advance function, no artifacts. **The CBB (ByteBuilder) cluster is a near-total re-derivation, not a spot-fix**: reading every body against its claim (rather than trusting the plausible per-content-type names) surfaced a coherent, self-consistent alternate mapping onto BoringSSL's actual small set of generic CBB lifecycle primitives — `0x600ecf48`=`CBB_zero` (disassembly-confirmed `movs r2,#0x10`=16-byte memset, not the claimed "36 bytes"), `0x600ecf50`=`CBB_init` (not "add_asn1_bitstring" — no bitstring logic exists, it allocates the buffer+header pair), `0x600ecf8c`=`CBB_cleanup` (not "add_asn1_bool" — no `0xff`/`0x00` write exists, it conditionally frees), `0x600ecfae`=`CBB_flush` (not "add_asn1_tag" — self-recursive, writes DER long-form length markers via `memmove`, called first by all 25 other functions matching `CBB_flush`'s universal role), `0x600ed09a`=`CBB_finish` (not "add_asn1_oid" — extracts data-ptr+length out-params then calls the real cleanup), `0x600ed0de`=`CBB_add_bytes` (not "add_asn1_null" — generic flush+reserve+`memcpy`, its 13 callers just happen to pass `len=0` for NULL-parameter call sites), `0x600ed10a`=`CBB_add_space` (not "add_bytes" — reserves and returns a write pointer but never calls `memcpy`, unlike `0x600ed0de`), `0x600ed148`=the internal `cbb_add_base128_integer`-shaped 7-bit-VLQ encoder (not "add_asn1_time" — no ASCII/decimal conversion anywhere, it's the confirmed real callee of `0x600ed1b4`'s high-tag-number path), and `0x600ed1b4`=`CBB_add_asn1` (not "CBB_finish" — writes low/high tag-number bytes and constructs a new child CBB, the real finish is `0x600ed09a`). Two further, independent findings: `0x600ed248` ("`crypto_cbb_add_u24`") disassembly-confirmed as `movs r3,#4` (4, not 3) — byte-identical in shape to the established `add_u8`/`add_u16` siblings with only the count literal changed, so this is `CBB_add_u32`, not `add_u24`; `0x600ed332` ("`crypto_cbb_add_asn1_bignum`") performs repeated ÷10 via `FUN_6004c814` — independently established in `bruce-crypto.md` as a `BN_div`-shaped divide primitive — extracting decimal-ASCII digits (`+ '0'`) into a stack buffer, i.e. an "itoa" formatter, not DER binary integer encoding (which `0x600ed266` in this same wave correctly implements as raw big-endian bytes). A tenth, softer finding: `0x600ed2e6` ("`crypto_cbs_get_u16`") takes its byte count as a runtime parameter and works for any width ≤ 8, so it's more likely the generic engine behind `CBS_get_u8`/`u16`/`u24`/`u32` than a fixed-16-bit-only reader. All 10 corrected/flagged in place in `analysis/bruce-misc-functions.md` with a summary note; no `GHIDRA-TODO` needed (identification-only, no boundary issues beyond the already-fixed `0x600ed380`). The other 10 rows — `crypto_cbb_add_u8`/`add_u16`/`add_asn1_uint64` and all 5 CBS functions (`cbs_init`/`cbs_skip`/`cbs_data`/`cbs_len`/`cbs_mem_equal`) — were read directly and are clean; `crypto_cbs_mem_equal`'s callee (`0x600e0976`) is a solid independent cross-session confirmation, matching its already-established session-88 identity (`crypto_constant_time_memcmp_diff`, a genuine no-early-exit XOR-accumulate constant-time comparator) exactly. **Reliability read**: this cluster's failure mode is a specific and instructive one — BoringSSL's real `cbb.c` genuinely has very few distinct primitives (`init`/`zero`/`cleanup`/`flush`/`add_asn1`/`finish`/`add_bytes`/`add_space`) that get called from *many* different ASN.1-content-writing call sites, so the wave's initial pass appears to have inferred each primitive's identity from a plausible-sounding *use-site* (a bitstring encoder calls `CBB_init`; an OID encoder calls `CBB_finish`) rather than from the primitive's own body — the same "assign identity from neighborhood/theme rather than from what the code actually computes" pattern flagged repeatedly in this crypto cluster since session 90, but at a larger scale (9-10 of 20 rows) than any prior single wave.

Reliability read: a substantial-finding wave, but one where the write-up's own "spurious split"/"fragment" language already signaled awareness — QA's contribution was byte-level proof, discovering the fragment extends further than claimed, and surfacing a deeper unresolved puzzle the write-up's framing didn't capture; the disconnect-handler finding continues this pipeline's familiar pattern of correct callee citations paired with a wrong verb/purpose for the enclosing function.
